<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 273. TAXES; LISTING, ASSESSMENT</h2>
      
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.01">
        <h1 class="shn">273.01 LISTING AND ASSESSMENT, TIME.</h1>
        <p>All real property subject to taxation shall be listed and at least one-fifth of the parcels listed shall be appraised each year with reference to their value on January 2 preceding the assessment so that each parcel shall be reappraised at maximum intervals of five years. All real property becoming taxable in any year shall be listed with reference to its value on January 2 of that year. Except as provided in this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/274.01#stat.274.01.1">274.01, subdivision 1</a>, all real property assessments shall be completed two weeks prior to the date scheduled for the local board of review or equalization. No changes in valuation or classification which are intended to correct errors in judgment by the county assessor may be made by the county assessor after the board of review or the county board of equalization has adjourned; however, corrections of errors for real or personal property that are merely clerical in nature or changes that extend homestead treatment to property are permitted after adjournment until the tax extension date for that assessment year. Any changes made by the assessor after adjournment must be fully documented and maintained in a file in the assessor's office and shall be available for review by any person. A copy of any changes made during this period shall be sent to the county board no later than December 31 of the assessment year. In the event a valuation and classification is not placed on any real property by the dates scheduled for the local board of review or equalization the valuation and classification determined in the preceding assessment shall be continued in effect and the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13">273.13</a> shall, in such case, not be applicable, except with respect to real estate which has been constructed since the previous assessment. Real property containing iron ore, the fee to which is owned by the state of Minnesota, shall, if leased by the state after January 2 in any year, be subject to assessment for that year on the value of any iron ore removed under said lease prior to January 2 of the following year. Personal property subject to taxation shall be listed and assessed annually with reference to its value on January 2; and, if acquired on that day, shall be listed by or for the person acquiring it.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.01.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/11">1984</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/11">RL s 802</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1945/0/485/#laws.0.1.0">1945 c 485 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/70/#laws.1.1.0">Ex1959 c 70 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/624/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 624 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/709/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 709 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/564/#laws.0.5.0">1971 c 564 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/437/#laws.8.2.0">1975 c 437 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.7.4.0">1988 c 719 art 7 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/277/#laws.2.16.0">1989 c 277 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.7.4.0">1990 c 480 art 7 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.2.10.0">2003 c 127 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.10.3.0">2014 c 308 art 10 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.011"><b>273.011</b> MS 1976 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/423/#laws.2.20.0">1977 c 423 art 2 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.012"><b>273.012</b> MS 1976 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/423/#laws.2.20.0">1977 c 423 art 2 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.273.015">
        <h1>273.015 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.015.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.015.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 1971 [Expired]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.02">
        <h1 class="shn">273.02 OMITTED PROPERTY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.02.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Discovery.</span></h2>
          <p>If any real or personal property be omitted in the assessment of any year or years, and the property thereby escape taxation, or if any real property be undervalued by reason of failure to take into consideration the existence of buildings or improvements thereon, or be erroneously classified as a homestead, when such omission, undervaluation or erroneous classification is discovered the county auditor shall in the case of omitted property enter such property on the assessment and tax books for the year or years omitted, and in the case of property undervalued by reason of failure to take into consideration the existence of buildings or improvements thereon, or property erroneously classified as a homestead, shall correct the net tax capacity or classification thereof on the assessment and tax books and shall assess the property, and extend against the same on the tax list for the current year all arrearage of taxes properly accruing against it, including therein, in the case of personal property taxes, interest thereon at the rate of seven percent per annum from the time such taxes would have become delinquent, when the omission was caused by the failure of the owner to list the same. If any tax on any property liable to taxation is prevented from being collected for any year or years by reason of any erroneous proceedings, undervaluation by reason of failure to take into consideration the existence of buildings or improvements, erroneous classification as a homestead, or other cause, the amount of such tax which such property should have paid shall be added to the tax on such property for the current year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.02.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in subdivisions 1 to 3 shall authorize the county auditor to enter omitted property on the assessment and tax books more than six years after the assessment date of the year in which the property was originally assessed or should have been assessed and nothing in subdivisions 1 to 3 shall authorize the county auditor to correct the net tax capacity or classification of real property as herein provided more than one year after December 1 of the year in which the property was assessed or should have been assessed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.02.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">What rights not affected.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in subdivisions 1 to 3 shall affect any rights in undervalued or erroneously classified property, acquired for value in good faith prior to the correction of the net tax capacity thereof by the county auditor as provided in this section. Any person whose rights are adversely affected by any action of the county auditor as provided in this subdivision may apply for a reduction of the net tax capacity under the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/375.192">375.192</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.02.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Iron ore.</span></h2>
          <p>Newly discovered iron ore shall be entered on the assessment books for the six years immediately preceding the year of discovery and taxed as omitted property. The tax on such omitted property shall be determined by applying the rates of levy for the respective years in which the property was omitted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.02.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Refunds for iron ore not found.</span></h2>
          <p>Any taxpayer having paid real estate taxes on valuations of iron ore, considered to be commercially mineable, which was believed to have existed, and was subsequently determined not to exist, may apply to the commissioner of revenue for a refund of taxes paid thereon, as provided herein. Such application for refund shall be filed in the year in which it is determined that the iron ore does not exist. No refund shall be made for taxes paid or payable more than six years previous to the date of said application. The refunds shall be paid from the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.02.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">General fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The taxes collected in accordance with subdivision 4 shall be transmitted by the county treasurer to the commissioner of management and budget and deposited in the general fund. There shall be paid from the general fund the amount of refunds determined in accordance with subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.02.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/11">1985</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/11">RL s 803</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/632/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 632 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1945/0/415/#laws.0.1.0">1945 c 415 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/624/#laws.0.7.0">1965 c 624 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/556/#laws.0.10.0">1974 c 556 s 10</a>-12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/423/#laws.10.1.0">1977 c 423 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/50/#laws.0.28.0">1979 c 50 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.84.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.13.20.0">1989 c 329 art 13 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.71.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 71</a>,72; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/471/#laws.3.4.0">1996 c 471 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.03">
        <h1 class="shn">273.03 REAL ESTATE; ASSESSMENT; METHOD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.03.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Assessment books.</span></h2>
          <p>The county auditor shall annually provide the necessary assessment books and blanks at the expense of the county, for and to correspond with each assessment district. The auditor shall make out, in the real property assessment book, complete lists of all lands or lots subject to taxation, showing the names of the owners, if known; and, if unknown, so stated opposite each tract or lot, the number of acres, and the lots or parts of lots or blocks, included in each description of property. The list of real property becoming subject to assessment and taxation may be appended to the personal property assessment book. The assessment books and blanks for real and personal property shall be in readiness for delivery to the assessors on or before the first Monday in December of each year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.03.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Election to use card ledger or electronic data system.</span></h2>
          <p>Any county in this state which employs a county assessor who maintains a unit card ledger system or similar system of real estate and the market value and net tax capacities ascertained by the assessor affecting such real estate, and which county has established an electronic data processing system or similar system to perform the processing of assessment and tax accounting, may discontinue the preparation of assessment books as provided in subdivision 1. The election to discontinue the preparation of assessment books as defined in subdivision 1 shall be made by the county auditor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.03.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.03.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/11">1986</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1905/0/86/#laws.0.0.0">1905 c 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1913/0/503/#laws.0.0.0">1913 c 503</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1917/0/297/#laws.0.0.0">1917 c 297</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1921/0/86/#laws.0.0.0">1921 c 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/331/#laws.0.1.0">1947 c 331 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/781/#laws.0.1.0">1963 c 781 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/624/#laws.0.2.0">1965 c 624 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/709/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 709 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/339/#laws.0.8.0">1975 c 339 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/437/#laws.8.3.0">1975 c 437 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/423/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 423 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.84.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.13.20.0">1989 c 329 art 13 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.3.11.0">1993 c 375 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/212/#laws.3.23.0">2006 c 212 art 3 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.032">
        <h1 class="shn">273.032 MARKET VALUE DEFINITION.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) Unless otherwise provided, for the purpose of determining any property tax levy limitation based on market value or any limit on net debt, the issuance of bonds, certificates of indebtedness, or capital notes based on market value, any qualification to receive state aid based on market value, or any state aid amount based on market value, the terms "market value," "estimated market value," and "market valuation," whether equalized or unequalized, mean the estimated market value of taxable property within the local unit of government before any of the following or similar adjustments for:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) the market value exclusions under:</p>
        <p class="in">(i) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.11">273.11, subdivisions 14a</a> and 14c (vacant platted land);</p>
        <p class="in">(ii) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.11">273.11, subdivisions 19</a> and 20 (certain improvements to business properties);</p>
        <p class="in">(iii) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.11#stat.273.11.21">273.11, subdivision 21</a> (homestead property damaged by mold);</p>
        <p class="in">(iv) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13#stat.273.13.34">273.13, subdivision 34</a> (homestead of a veteran with a disability or family caregiver); or</p>
        <p class="in">(v) section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13#stat.273.13.35">273.13, subdivision 35</a> (homestead market value exclusion); or</p>
        <p class="in">(2) the deferment of value under:</p>
        <p class="in">(i) the Minnesota Agricultural Property Tax Law, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.111">273.111</a>;</p>
        <p class="in">(ii) the Aggregate Resource Preservation Law, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1115">273.1115</a>;</p>
        <p class="in">(iii) the Minnesota Open Space Property Tax Law, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.112">273.112</a>;</p>
        <p class="in">(iv) the rural preserves property tax program, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.114">273.114</a>; or</p>
        <p class="in">(v) the Metropolitan Agricultural Preserves Act, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.10">473H.10</a>; or</p>
        <p class="in">(3) the adjustments to tax capacity for:</p>
        <p class="in">(i) tax increment financing under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.174">469.174</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.1794">469.1794</a>;</p>
        <p class="in">(ii) fiscal disparities under chapter 276A or 473F; or</p>
        <p class="in">(iii) powerline credit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.425">273.425</a>.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Estimated market value under paragraph (a) also includes the market value of tax-exempt property if the applicable law specifically provides that the limitation, qualification, or aid calculation includes tax-exempt property.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) Unless otherwise provided, "market value," "estimated market value," and "market valuation" for purposes of property tax levy limitations and calculation of state aid, refer to the estimated market value for the previous assessment year and for purposes of limits on net debt, the issuance of bonds, certificates of indebtedness, or capital notes refer to the estimated market value as last finally equalized.</p>
        <p class="in">(d) For purposes of a provision of a home rule charter or of any special law that is not codified in the statutes and that imposes a levy limitation based on market value or any limit on debt, the issuance of bonds, certificates of indebtedness, or capital notes based on market value, the terms "market value," "taxable market value," and "market valuation," whether equalized or unequalized, mean "estimated market value" as defined in paragraph (a).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.032.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/416/#laws.1.12.0">1994 c 416 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/31/#laws.3.3.0">1997 c 31 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/259/#laws.9.5.0">2006 c 259 art 9 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/13/#laws.1.12.0">2007 c 13 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.14.25.0">2013 c 143 art 14 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.157.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 157</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.24.3.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 24 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.85.0">2024 c 85 s 85</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.04"><b>273.04</b> MS 1982 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/593/#laws.0.46.0">1984 c 593 s 46</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.05">
        <h1 class="shn">273.05 ASSESSORS; APPOINTMENT, TERM, AND OATH.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.05.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Appointment of town and city assessors.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other provision of law all town assessors shall be appointed by the town board, and notwithstanding any charter provisions to the contrary, all city assessors shall be appointed by the city council or other appointing authority as provided by law or charter. They shall be selected and appointed because of their knowledge and training in the field of property taxation. All town and statutory city assessors shall be appointed for indefinite terms. A town or statutory city assessor who is an employee may be dismissed by the appointing authority for cause. The term of the town or city assessors may be terminated at any time by the town board or city council on charges by the commissioner of revenue of inefficiency or neglect of duty. Vacancies in the office of town or city assessor shall be filled within 90 days by appointment of the respective appointing authority indicated above. If the vacancy is not filled within 90 days, the office shall be terminated. When a vacancy in the office of town or city assessor is not filled by appointment, and it is imperative that the office of assessor be filled, the county auditor shall appoint some resident of the county as assessor for such town or city. The county auditor may appoint the county assessor as assessor for such town or city, in which case the town or city shall pay to the county treasurer the amount determined by the county auditor to be due for the services performed and expenses incurred by the county assessor in acting as assessor for such town or city. The term of any town or statutory city assessor in a county electing in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.052">273.052</a> shall be terminated as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.055">273.055</a>.</p>
          <p>The commissioner of revenue may recommend to the state board of assessors the nonrenewal, suspension, or revocation of an assessor's license as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270.41">270.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270.50">270.50</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.05.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Oath of assessors.</span></h2>
          <p>Every person elected or appointed to the office of assessor, at or before the time of receiving the assessment books, shall take and subscribe an oath to be diligent, faithful, and impartial in performance of the duties enjoined on the assessor by law. Failure to take the oath within the time prescribed shall be deemed a refusal to serve.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.05.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cities and townships; employment of licensed assessor.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">In the case of cities or townships, except cities or towns located in Ramsey County or which have elected a county assessor system in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.055">273.055</a>, the commissioner shall allow the city or town 90 days from the date of incorporation or organization to employ a licensed assessor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.05.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/11">1987</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/11">RL s 805</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/799/#laws.0.1.0">1963 c 799 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/254/#laws.0.2.0">1965 c 254 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/282/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 282 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/823/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 823 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/989/#laws.0.6.0">1969 c 989 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/434/#laws.0.7.0">1977 c 434 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/593/#laws.0.12.0">1984 c 593 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.7.5.0">1988 c 719 art 7 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/441/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 441 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.5.11.0">2003 c 127 art 5 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.13.24.0">2008 c 154 art 13 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 50</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.051">
        <h1 class="shn">273.051 CITY ASSESSORS, TERM.</h1>
        <p>The term of elected city assessors shall not expire until a vacancy occurs in the office or upon the completion of the present term for which an assessor is elected. Thereafter the term of such city assessors shall be for the period provided in the charter. The terms of all other city assessors shall continue as provided by charter or as otherwise provided by statute. The term of any city assessor in a county electing in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.052">273.052</a> shall be terminated as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.055">273.055</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.051.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/254/#laws.0.3.0">1965 c 254 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/989/#laws.0.7.0">1969 c 989 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.052">
        <h1 class="shn">273.052 APPOINTMENT; APPLICATION.</h1>
        <p>Any county in the state of Minnesota, notwithstanding any other provision of law to the contrary, is hereby authorized and empowered to provide for the assessment of all taxable property in the county by the county assessor.</p>
        <p>This section shall not apply to Ramsey County, or property assessable in cities whose assessor has the powers of a county assessor pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.063">273.063</a>, or property which is by law assessed by the commissioner of revenue.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.052.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/989/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 989 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/435/#laws.5.1.0">1974 c 435 art 5 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.053">
        <h1 class="shn">273.053 ASSESSMENT; EXPENSES.</h1>
        <p>Any county electing in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.052">273.052</a> is authorized and empowered to appropriate sufficient money to defray the expenses of making a proper assessment of all property in such county for the purpose of general taxation. The county board shall by resolution authorize the county assessor to employ such additional deputies, clerks, field workers, appraisers, and employees as it may deem necessary for the proper performance of the duties of the office of county assessor; such expenditure to include the hiring of experts in property valuation for any period deemed necessary, the payment of the transportation expense of such experts or other employees in traveling from place to place in the county, and generally any expense reasonably and directly tending to the procurement of a fair and true assessment of property within such county; but all such shall be made under the supervision of, and with the consent of, the county assessor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.053.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/989/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 989 s 2</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.0535">
        <h1 class="shn">273.0535 COUNTY OR LOCAL ASSESSING DISTRICT TO ASSUME COST OF TRAINING.</h1>
        <p class="in">The county or local assessing district must assume the cost of training its assessors in courses approved by the board for the purpose of obtaining the assessor's license to the extent of course fees, mileage, meals, and lodging, and recognized travel expenses not paid by the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.0535.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.13.25.0">2008 c 154 art 13 s 25</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.054">
        <h1 class="shn">273.054 DUTIES AND POWERS OF ASSESSOR.</h1>
        <p>A county assessor appointed in an electing county shall have all the duties and powers provided by statute, except those inconsistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.05">273.05</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.051">273.051</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.052">273.052</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.053">273.053</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.055">273.055</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.056">273.056</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.054.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/989/#laws.0.3.0">1969 c 989 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.40.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.055">
        <h1 class="shn">273.055 RESOLUTION TO APPOINT ASSESSOR; TERMINATION OF LOCAL ASSESSOR'S OFFICE.</h1>
        <p>The election to provide for the assessment of property by the county assessor as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.052">273.052</a> shall be made by the board of county commissioners by resolution. Such resolution shall be effective at the second assessment date following the adoption of the resolution. Notwithstanding any other provisions contained in any other section of law or charter, the office of all township and city assessors in such county shall be terminated 90 days before the assessment date at which the election becomes effective, except that if part of such taxing district is located in a county not electing to have the county assessor assess all property as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.052">273.052</a>, the office will continue but shall apply only to such property in a nonelecting county.</p>
        <p>No township or city assessor in another county shall assess any property in an electing county, but shall turn over all tax records relating to property to the county assessor 90 days before the assessment date at which the county's election becomes effective.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.055.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/989/#laws.0.4.0">1969 c 989 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.056">
        <h1 class="shn">273.056 REVOCATION OF COUNTY ASSESSOR'S ELECTION; LOCAL ASSESSORS.</h1>
        <p>If after electing in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.055">273.055</a>, the board of county commissioners shall determine that the interests of the county may be better served through valuation by local assessors, it may revoke the election. Such revocation may not be made within four years after the election. In the event of revocation, it shall be effective at the second assessment date following such revocation. The offices of all township and city assessors shall be filled as provided by charter or law 90 days before such effective date.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.056.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/989/#laws.0.5.0">1969 c 989 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.06">
        <h1 class="shn">273.06 DEPUTY ASSESSORS.</h1>
        <p>Any assessor who deems it necessary to complete the listing and valuation of the property of the town or district within the time prescribed, with the approbation of the county auditor, may appoint a well-qualified citizen of the town or district to act as assistant or deputy, and may assign to that person such portion of the district as the assessor thinks proper. Each assistant so appointed, after taking the required oath, shall perform, under the direction of the assessor, all the duties imposed upon assessors by this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/11">1988</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/11">RL s 806</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/434/#laws.0.9.0">1977 c 434 s 9</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.061">
        <h1 class="shn">273.061 ESTABLISHMENT OF OFFICE FOR EACH COUNTY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Office created; appointment, qualifications.</span></h2>
          <p>Every county in this state shall have a county assessor. The county assessor shall be appointed by the board of county commissioners. The assessor shall be selected and appointed because of knowledge and training in the field of property taxation and appointment shall be approved by the commissioner of revenue before the same shall become effective. Upon receipt by the county commissioners of the commissioner of revenue's refusal to approve an appointment, the term of the appointee shall terminate at the end of that day.</p>
          <p>The commissioner of revenue may grant approval on a probationary basis for a period of two years. The commissioner must base the decision to impose a probationary period on objective and consistent criteria. At the end of the two-year probationary period, the commissioner may either refuse to approve the person's appointment for the remainder of the person's four-year term, approve the person's appointment but only for another two-year probationary period, or unconditionally approve the person's appointment for the remainder of the four-year term for which the person was originally appointed by the county board. The criteria shall not be considered rules and are not subject to the Administrative Procedure Act.</p>
          <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, a county assessor must have senior accreditation from the state Board of Assessors by January 1, 1992, or within two years of the assessor's first appointment under this section, whichever is later.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Compatible offices.</span></h2>
          <p>A person appointed as the county assessor also may serve as the county auditor, county treasurer, or county auditor-treasurer if those offices are appointive, provided that the person in the combined appointed office must not serve on the county board of appeal and equalization under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/274.13">274.13</a>. In a county in which the functions of the county assessor are combined with those of the county auditor or county auditor-treasurer, the county board may not delegate any authority, power, or responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/375.192#stat.375.192.4">375.192, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Compatible offices in counties changing to appointed auditor.</span></h2>
          <p>In a county in which the office of auditor, treasurer, or auditor-treasurer is an elective position, a person appointed as the county assessor also may serve as the county auditor, county treasurer, or county auditor-treasurer if a proposal to make the affected office appointive has been approved as required by other law and will be effective within five years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.1c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1c.<span class="headnote">Incompatible offices.</span></h2>
          <p>The person appointed as the county assessor must not also be the county attorney, a county board member, an elected county auditor, an elected county treasurer, an elected county auditor-treasurer, a town board supervisor for a town in the same county, or a city mayor or council member for a city in the same county. The person appointed as the city assessor must not also be a city council member or mayor for the same city. A person appointed as the town assessor must not also be a town board supervisor for the same town. Except as provided in subdivision 1b, an assessor who accepts a position that is incompatible with the office of assessor is deemed to have resigned from the assessor position.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Term; vacancy.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The terms of county assessors appointed under this section shall be four years. A new term shall begin on January 1 of every fourth year after 1973. When any vacancy in the office occurs, the board of county commissioners, within 90 days thereafter, shall fill the same by appointment for the remainder of the term, following the procedure prescribed in subdivision 1. The term of the county assessor may be terminated by the board of county commissioners at any time, on charges of malfeasance, misfeasance, or nonfeasance by the commissioner of revenue. If the board of county commissioners does not intend to reappoint a county assessor who has been certified by the state Board of Assessors, the board shall present written notice to the county assessor not later than 90 days prior to the termination of the assessor's term, that it does not intend to reappoint the assessor. If written notice is not timely made, the county assessor will automatically be reappointed by the board of county commissioners.</p>
          <p>(b) In the event of a vacancy in the office of county assessor, through death, resignation or other reasons, the deputy (or chief deputy, if more than one) shall perform the functions of the office. If there is no deputy, the county auditor shall designate a person to perform the duties of the office until an appointment is made as provided in clause (a). Such person shall perform the duties of the office for a period not exceeding 90 days during which the county board must appoint a county assessor. Such 90-day period may, however, be extended by written approval of the commissioner of revenue.</p>
          <p>(c) In the case of the first appointment under paragraph (a) of a county assessor who is accredited but who does not have senior accreditation, an approval of the appointment by the commissioner shall be provisional, provided that a county assessor appointed to a provisional term under this paragraph must reapply to the commissioner at the end of the provisional term. A provisional term may not exceed two years. The commissioner shall not approve the appointment for the remainder of the four-year term unless the assessor has obtained senior accreditation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Oath.</span></h2>
          <p>Every county assessor, before entering upon duties, shall take and subscribe the oath required of public officials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Assistants.</span></h2>
          <p>With the approval of the board of county commissioners, the county assessor may employ one or more assistants and sufficient clerical help to perform the duties of the assessor's office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Offices; supplies.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of county commissioners shall provide suitable office space and equipment at the county seat for the county assessor, assistants and clerical help, and shall furnish such books, maps, stationery, postage and supplies as may be necessary for the discharge of the duties of the office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Salaries; expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>The salaries of the county assessor and assistants and clerical help, shall be fixed by the board of county commissioners and shall be payable out of the general revenue fund of the county.</p>
          <p>In addition to their salaries, the county assessor and assistants shall be allowed their expenses for reasonable and necessary travel in the performance of their duties, including necessary travel, lodging and meal expense incurred by them while attending meetings of instructions or official hearings called by the commissioner of revenue. These expenses shall be payable out of the general revenue fund of the county, and shall be allowed on the same basis as such expenses are allowed to other county officers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Division of duties between local and county assessor.</span></h2>
          <p>The duty of the duly appointed local assessor shall be to view and appraise the value of all property as provided by law, but all the book work shall be done by the county assessor, or the assessor's assistants, and the value of all property subject to assessment and taxation shall be determined by the county assessor, except as otherwise hereinafter provided. If directed by the county assessor, the local assessor must perform the duties enumerated in subdivision 8, clause (16), and must enter construction and valuation data into the records in the manner prescribed by the county assessor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Powers and duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The county assessor shall have the following powers and duties:</p>
          <p>(1) To call upon and confer with the township and city assessors in the county, and advise and give them the necessary instructions and directions as to their duties under the laws of this state, to the end that a uniform assessment of all real property in the county will be attained.</p>
          <p>(2) To assist and instruct the local assessors in the preparation and proper use of land maps and record cards, in the property classification of real and personal property, and in the determination of proper standards of value.</p>
          <p>(3) To keep the local assessors in the county advised of all changes in assessment laws and all instructions which the assessor receives from the commissioner of revenue relating to their duties.</p>
          <p>(4) To have authority to require the attendance of groups of local assessors at sectional meetings called by the assessor for the purpose of giving them further assistance and instruction as to their duties.</p>
          <p>(5) To immediately commence the preparation of a large scale topographical land map of the county, in such form as may be prescribed by the commissioner of revenue, showing thereon the location of all railroads, highways and roads, bridges, rivers and lakes, swamp areas, wooded tracts, stony ridges and other features which might affect the value of the land. Appropriate symbols shall be used to indicate the best, the fair, and the poor land of the county. For use in connection with the topographical land map, the assessor shall prepare and keep available in the assessor's office tables showing fair average minimum and maximum market values per acre of cultivated, meadow, pasture, cutover, timber and waste lands of each township. The assessor shall keep the map and tables available in the office for the guidance of town assessors, boards of review, and the county board of equalization.</p>
          <p>(6) To also prepare and keep available in the office for the guidance of town assessors, boards of review and the county board of equalization, a land valuation map of the county, in such form as may be prescribed by the commissioner of revenue. This map, which shall include the bordering tier of townships of each county adjoining, shall show the average market value per acre, both with and without improvements, as finally equalized in the last assessment of real estate, of all land in each town or unorganized township which lies outside the corporate limits of cities.</p>
          <p>(7) To regularly examine all conveyances of land outside the corporate limits of cities of the first and second class, filed with the county recorder of the county, and keep a file, by descriptions, of the considerations shown thereon. From the information obtained by comparing the considerations shown with the market values assessed, the assessor shall make recommendations to the county board of equalization of necessary changes in individual assessments or aggregate valuations.</p>
          <p>(8) To become familiar with the values of the different items of personal property so as to be in a position when called upon to advise the boards of review and the county board of equalization concerning property, market values thereof.</p>
          <p>(9) While the county board of equalization is in session, to give it every possible assistance to enable it to perform its duties. The assessor shall furnish the board with all necessary charts, tables, comparisons, and data which it requires in its deliberations, and shall make whatever investigations the board may desire.</p>
          <p>(10) At the request of either the board of county commissioners or the commissioner of revenue, to investigate applications for reductions of valuation and abatements and settlements of taxes, examine the real or personal property involved, and submit written reports and recommendations with respect to the applications, in such form as may be prescribed by the board of county commissioners and commissioner of revenue.</p>
          <p>(11) To make diligent search each year for real and personal property which has been omitted from assessment in the county, and report all such omissions to the county auditor.</p>
          <p>(12) To regularly confer with county assessors in all adjacent counties about the assessment of property in order to uniformly assess and equalize the value of similar properties and classes of property located in adjacent counties. The conference shall emphasize the assessment of agricultural and commercial and industrial property or other properties that may have an inadequate number of sales in a single county.</p>
          <p>(13) To render such other services pertaining to the assessment of real and personal property in the county as are not inconsistent with the duties set forth in this section, and as may be required by the board of county commissioners or by the commissioner of revenue.</p>
          <p>(14) To maintain a record, in conjunction with other county offices, of all transfers of property to assist in determining the proper classification of property, including but not limited to, transferring homestead property and name changes on homestead property.</p>
          <p>(15) To determine if a homestead application is required due to the transfer of homestead property or an owner's name change on homestead property.</p>
          <p>(16) To perform appraisals of property, review the original assessment and determine the accuracy of the original assessment, prepare an appraisal or appraisal report, and testify before any court or other body as an expert or otherwise on behalf of the assessor's jurisdiction with respect to properties in that jurisdiction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.8a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.<span class="headnote">Additional powers and duties of the commissioner of revenue, county assessors and local assessors.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any provision of law to the contrary, in order to promote a uniform assessment and review of assessments, the commissioner of revenue, county assessors and local assessors may exchange data on property which are classified under chapter 13 as public, nonpublic or private. The data for any property may include but is not limited to its sales, income, expenses, vacancies, rentable or usable areas, anticipated income and expenses, projected vacancies, lease information, and private multiple listing service data. Data exchanged under this provision that is classified as nonpublic or private data shall retain its classification.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Additional general duties.</span></h2>
          <p>Additional duties of the county assessor are as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) to make all assessments, based upon the appraised values reported by the local assessors or assistants and the county assessor's own knowledge of the value of the property assessed;</p>
          <p>(2) to personally view and determine the value of any property that because of its type or character may be difficult for the local assessor to appraise;</p>
          <p>(3) to make all changes ordered by the local boards of review, relative to the net tax capacity of the property of any individual, firm or corporation after notice has been given and hearings held as provided by law;</p>
          <p>(4) to enter all assessments in the assessment books, furnished by the county auditor, with each book and the tabular statements for each book in correct balance;</p>
          <p>(5) to prepare all assessment cards, charts, maps and any other forms prescribed by the commissioner of revenue;</p>
          <p>(6) to attend the meeting of the county board of equalization; to investigate and report on any assessment ordered by said board; to enter all changes made by said board in the assessment books and prepare information reported to the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.85#stat.270C.85.2">270C.85, subdivision 2</a>, clause (4); to enter all changes made by the State Board of Equalization in the assessment books; to deduct all exemptions authorized by law from each assessment and certify to the county auditor the taxable value of each parcel of land, as described and listed in the assessment books by the county auditor, and the taxable value of the personal property of each person, firm, or corporation assessed;</p>
          <p>(7) to investigate and make recommendations relative to all applications for the abatement of taxes or applications for the reduction of the net tax capacity of any property; and</p>
          <p>(8) to perform all other duties relating to the assessment of property for the purpose of taxation which may be required by the commissioner of revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Assessor in unorganized territory.</span></h2>
          <p>In counties having unorganized territory divided into one or more assessment districts, the board of county commissioners may appoint the county assessor for all such districts. In such case the assessor shall receive no compensation for performing the duties of assessor. The assessor shall, however, be allowed expenses for reasonable and necessary travel in the performance of duties. Such expenses shall be payable out of the general revenue fund of the county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.061.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>MS 1980 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/4/#laws.1.189.0">1Sp1981 c 4 art 1 s 189</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.061.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/32/#laws.8.1.0">Ex1967 c 32 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/9/#laws.0.68.0">1969 c 9 s 68</a>,69; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/498/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 498 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/434/#laws.0.4.0">1971 c 434 s 4,5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/18/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 18 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/567/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 567 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/301/#laws.0.3.0">1975 c 301 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/339/#laws.0.8.0">1975 c 339 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/437/#laws.1.32.0">1975 c 437 art 1 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/181/#laws.0.2.0">1976 c 181 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/434/#laws.0.10.0">1977 c 434 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/50/#laws.0.30.0">1979 c 50 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/423/#laws.0.5.0">1980 c 423 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/593/#laws.0.13.0">1984 c 593 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.7.28.0">1987 c 268 art 7 s 28</a>-30; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.84.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 84</a>; art 7 s 6,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/277/#laws.2.17.0">1989 c 277 art 2 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.13.20.0">1989 c 329 art 13 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.3.6.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.3.12.0">1993 c 375 art 3 s 12</a>; art 5 s 7; art 11 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.5.2.0">1994 c 587 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.7.14.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 7 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.5.12.0">2003 c 127 art 5 s 12</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.51.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/354/#laws.0.3.0">2010 c 354 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.4.15.0">2013 c 143 art 4 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.25.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.15.14.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 15 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.18.5.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 18 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.062">
        <h1 class="shn">273.062 VALUATION AND ASSESSMENT OF PERSONAL PROPERTY.</h1>
        <p>The county assessor, or city assessor in a city with population of 30,000 or more shall value and assess all personal property. The assessor shall make an alphabetical list of the names of all persons in the town or district liable to an assessment of personal property, and shall call at the office or place of business or residence of each person required by this chapter to list property, and shall list the person's name, and shall require each person to make and deliver a correct list and statement of such property, according to the prescribed form, which shall be subscribed and sworn to by the person listing; and the assessor shall thereupon determine the value of the property in such statement, and enter the same in the assessment books, opposite the name of the person assessed, with the name and post office address of the person listing the property; and, if such person reside in a city, the street and number, or other brief description, of the person's residence or place of business. If any property is listed or assessed on or after the last Monday in February, and before the return of the assessor's books, the same shall be as legal and binding as if listed and assessed before that time.</p>
        <p>Such county or city assessor shall have power and authority to summon witnesses to appear and give testimony, and to produce books, records, papers and documents relating to the listing of personal property.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.062.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/32/#laws.8.9.0">Ex1967 c 32 art 8 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/709/#laws.0.3.0">1969 c 709 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.063">
        <h1 class="shn">273.063 APPLICATION; LIMITATIONS.</h1>
        <p>The provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.161">272.161</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.061">273.061</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.062">273.062</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.063">273.063</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.072">273.072</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.08">273.08</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.10">273.10</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/274.01">274.01</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/375.192">375.192 </a>shall apply to all counties except Ramsey County. The following limitations shall apply as to the extent of the county assessors jurisdiction:</p>
        <p>In counties having a city of the first class, the powers and duties of the county assessor within such city shall be performed by the duly appointed city assessor. In all other cities having a population of 30,000 persons or more, according to the last preceding federal census, except in counties having a county assessor on January 1, 1967, the powers and duties of the county assessor within such cities shall be performed by the duly appointed city assessor, provided that the county assessor shall retain the supervisory duties contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.061#stat.273.061.8">273.061, subdivision 8</a>. For purposes of this section, "powers and duties" means the powers and duties identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.061#stat.273.061.8">273.061, subdivision 8</a>, clauses (5) to (16).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.063.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/32/#laws.8.10.0">Ex1967 c 32 art 8 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/435/#laws.5.2.0">1974 c 435 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.41.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.13.6.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 13 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.064">
        <h1 class="shn">273.064 EXAMINATION OF LOCAL ASSESSOR'S WORK; COMPLETION OF ASSESSMENTS.</h1>
        <p>The county assessor shall examine the assessment appraisal records of each local assessor anytime after December 1 of each year and shall immediately give notice in writing to the governing body of said district of any deficiencies in the assessment procedures with respect to the quantity of or quality of the work done as of that date and indicating corrective measures to be undertaken and effected by the local assessor not later than 30 days thereafter. If, upon reexamination of such records at that time, the deficiencies noted in the written notice previously given have not been substantially corrected to the end that a timely and uniform assessment of all real property in the county will be attained, then the county assessor with the approval of the county board shall collect the necessary records from the local assessor and complete the assessment or employ others to complete the assessment. When the county assessor has completed the assessments, the local assessor shall thereafter resume the assessment function within the district. In this circumstance the cost of completing the assessment shall be charged against the assessment district involved. The county auditor shall certify the costs thus incurred to the appropriate governing body not later than August 1 and if unpaid as of September 1 of the assessment year, the county auditor shall levy a tax upon the taxable property of said assessment district sufficient to pay such costs. The amount so collected shall be credited to the general revenue fund of the county.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.064.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/434/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 434 s 1,5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.9.19.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 9 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.3.8.0">1990 c 604 art 3 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.0645">
        <h1 class="shn">273.0645 COMMISSIONER REVIEW OF ASSESSMENT PRACTICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.0645.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Local assessment practices.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of revenue must review the assessment practices in a taxing jurisdiction if requested in writing by a qualifying number of property owners in that taxing jurisdiction. The request must be signed by the greater of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) ten percent of the registered voters who voted in the last general election; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) five property owners.</p>
          <p class="in">The request must identify the city, town, or county and describe why a review is sought for that taxing jurisdiction. The commissioner must conduct the review in a reasonable amount of time and report the findings to the county board of the affected county, to the affected city council or town board, if the review is for a specific city or town, and to the property owner designated in the request as the person to receive the report on behalf of all the property owners who signed the request. The commissioner must also provide the report electronically to all property owners who signed the request and provided an email address in order to receive the report electronically.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.0645.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Nonfeasance, misfeasance, and malfeasance.</span></h2>
          <p>County assessors may file a written complaint with the commissioner of revenue detailing allegations of nonfeasance, misfeasance, or malfeasance by a local assessor. After receiving a complaint from a county assessor, the commissioner must complete an investigation and recommend an appropriate action to the State Board of Assessors. The commissioner is not required to have a written complaint from a county assessor in order to conduct an investigation and recommend an action to the board. Active investigative data relating to the investigation of complaints against an assessor by the commissioner of revenue are subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.39">13.39</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.0645.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.6.8.0">2008 c 366 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.4.16.0">2013 c 143 art 4 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.065">
        <h1 class="shn">273.065 DELIVERY OF ASSESSMENT APPRAISAL RECORDS; EXTENSIONS.</h1>
        <p>Assessment districts shall complete the assessment appraisal records on or before February 1. The records shall be delivered to the county assessor as of that date and any work which is the responsibility of the local assessor which is not completed by February 1 shall be accomplished by the county assessor or persons employed by the county assessor and the cost of such work shall be charged against the assessment district as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.064">273.064</a>. Extensions of time to complete the assessment appraisal records may be granted to the local assessor by the county assessor if such extension is approved by the county board.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.065.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/434/#laws.0.2.0">1971 c 434 s 2,5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.7.31.0">1987 c 268 art 7 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.9.20.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 9 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.07"><b>273.07</b> MS 1945 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/531/#laws.0.10.0">1947 c 531 s 10</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.071"><b>273.071</b> MS 1965 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/32/#laws.8.12.0">Ex1967 c 32 art 8 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.072">
        <h1 class="shn">273.072 AGREEMENTS FOR JOINT ASSESSMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.072.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Joint assessment agreements.</span></h2>
          <p>Any county and any city or town lying wholly or partially within the county and constituting a separate assessment district may, by agreement entered into under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, provide for the assessment of property in the municipality or town by the county assessor. Any two or more cities or towns constituting separate assessment districts may enter into an agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> for the assessment of property in the contracting units by the assessor of one of the units or by an assessor who is jointly employed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.072.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Abolishment of office of local assessor.</span></h2>
          <p>The agreement may provide for the abolition of the office of local assessor in any contracting unit when the assessment of property within it is to be made under the agreement by another assessor. In such case, the office of assessor in that unit shall cease to exist upon the date fixed in the agreement but not before the end of the term of the incumbent, if serving for a fixed term, or when an earlier vacancy occurs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.072.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Agreement terms.</span></h2>
          <p>When the agreement provides for joint employment of an assessor, the assessor shall be appointed and removed in a manner and shall hold office for such term as is provided in the agreement, notwithstanding charter or other statutory provisions for election or appointment of an assessor for a prescribed term.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.072.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Termination of agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>If the agreement is for an indefinite term, it may be terminated on six months notice by either party. Upon the termination of the agreement, whether for a fixed or indefinite term, any office of assessor abolished as a result of the agreement shall be automatically reestablished and shall be filled as provided by applicable law or charter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.072.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Payment; county general fund.</span></h2>
          <p>Any amount paid to the county for personal services of the county assessor under such an agreement shall be paid into the general revenue fund of the county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.072.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Powers of local boards of review not affected.</span></h2>
          <p>Agreements made under this section have no effect upon the powers and duties of local boards of review and equalization.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.072.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/382/#laws.0.1.0">1959 c 382 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/32/#laws.8.5.0">Ex1967 c 32 art 8 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/1/#laws.4.11.0">1Sp1986 c 1 art 4 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.7.16.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 7 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.075"><b>273.075</b> MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.0755">
        <h1 class="shn">273.0755 TRAINING AND EDUCATION OF PROPERTY TAX PERSONNEL.</h1>
        <p>(a) Beginning with the four-year period starting on July 1, 2020, every person licensed by the state Board of Assessors at the Accredited Minnesota Assessor level or higher, shall successfully complete 30 hours of educational coursework on Minnesota laws, assessment administration, and administrative procedures sponsored by the Department of Revenue in every four-year period.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner of revenue may require that each county, and each city for which the city assessor performs the duties of county assessor, have (1) a person on the assessor's staff who is certified by the Department of Revenue in sales ratio calculations, (2) an officer or employee who is certified by the Department of Revenue in tax calculations, and (3) an officer or employee who is certified by the Department of Revenue in the proper preparation of information reported to the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.85#stat.270C.85.2">270C.85, subdivision 2</a>, clause (4). Certifications under this paragraph expire after four years.</p>
        <p>(c) Beginning with the four-year educational licensing period starting on July 1, 2004, every Minnesota assessor licensed by the State Board of Assessors must attend and participate in a seminar that focuses on ethics, professional conduct and the need for standardized assessment practices developed and presented by the commissioner of revenue. This requirement must be met at least once in every subsequent four-year period. This requirement applies to all assessors licensed for one year or more in the four-year period.</p>
        <p>(d) When the commissioner of revenue determines that an individual or board that performs functions related to property tax administration has performed those functions in a manner that is not uniform or equitable, the commissioner may require that the individual or members of the board complete supplemental training. The commissioner may not require that an individual complete more than 32 hours of supplemental training pursuant to this paragraph. If the individual is required to complete supplemental training due to that individual's membership on a local or county board of appeal and equalization, the commissioner may not require that the individual complete more than two hours of supplemental training.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.0755.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.7.17.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 7 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.1.7.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.20.5.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 20 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.18.6.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 18 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.13.7.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 13 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.08">
        <h1 class="shn">273.08 ASSESSOR'S DUTIES.</h1>
        <p>The assessor shall actually view, and determine the market value of each tract or lot of real property listed for taxation, including the value of all improvements and structures thereon, at maximum intervals of five years and shall enter the value opposite each description. When directed by the county assessor, local assessors must enter construction and valuation data into the records in the manner prescribed by the county assessor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.08.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/11">1990</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/11">RL s 808</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1945/0/481/#laws.0.1.0">1945 c 481 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/799/#laws.0.2.0">1963 c 799 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/624/#laws.0.4.0">1965 c 624 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/32/#laws.8.7.0">Ex1967 c 32 art 8 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/437/#laws.8.9.0">1975 c 437 art 8 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/593/#laws.0.14.0">1984 c 593 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.2.11.0">2003 c 127 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.15.15.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 15 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.09"><b>273.09</b> [Local, South St. Paul]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.10">
        <h1 class="shn">273.10 SCHOOL DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <p>When assessing personal property the county assessor shall designate the number of the school district in which each person assessed is liable for tax. When the personal property of any person is assessable in several school districts, the amount in each shall be assessed separately, and the name of the owner placed opposite each amount.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.10.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/11">1991</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/11">RL s 809</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/32/#laws.8.8.0">Ex1967 c 32 art 8 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.26.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.105">
        <h1 class="shn">273.105 INTERNAL REVENUE CODE.</h1>
        <p class="in">Unless specifically defined otherwise, for purposes of this chapter, "Internal Revenue Code" means the Internal Revenue Code as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.02#stat.289A.02.7">289A.02, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.105.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.11.9.0">2008 c 366 art 11 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.11">
        <h1 class="shn">273.11 VALUATION OF PROPERTY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.11.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Except as provided in this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.17#stat.273.17.1">273.17, subdivision 1</a>, all property shall be valued at its market value. The market value as determined pursuant to this section shall be stated such that any amount under $100 is rounded up to $100 and any amount exceeding $100 shall be rounded to the nearest $100. In estimating and determining such value, the assessor shall not adopt a lower or different standard of value because the same is to serve as a basis of taxation, nor shall the assessor adopt as a criterion of value the price for which such property would sell at a forced sale, or in the aggregate with all the property in the town or district; but the assessor shall value each article or description of property by itself, and at such sum or price as the assessor believes the same to be fairly worth in money.
