The termination is effective upon adoption of a resolution of the county board. A county has 60 days from receipt of the first application for enrollment under this section to notify the applicant and any subsequent applicants of the county's intent to begin the process of terminating application of this section in the county. The county must act on the termination within six months. Upon termination by a vote of the county board, all applications received prior to and during notification of intent to terminate shall be deemed void. If the county board does not act on the termination within six months of notification, all applications for valuation for deferment received shall be deemed eligible for consideration to be enrolled under this section. Following this initial 60-day grace period, a termination applies prospectively and does not affect property enrolled under this section prior to the termination date. A county may reauthorize application of this section by a resolution of the county board revoking the termination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1115.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Additional taxes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When real property which has been valued and assessed under this section no longer qualifies, the portion of the land classified under subdivision 2, clause (1), is subject to additional taxes. The additional tax amount is determined by:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) computing the difference between (i) the current year's taxes determined in accordance with subdivision 4, and (ii) an amount as determined by the assessor based upon the property's current year's estimated market value of like real estate at its highest and best use and the appropriate local tax rate; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) multiplying the amount determined in clause (1) by the number of years the land was in the program under this section. The current year's estimated market value as determined by the assessor must not exceed the market value that would result if the property was sold in an arms-length transaction and must not be greater than it would have been had the actual bona fide sale price of the property been used in lieu of that market value. The additional taxes must be extended against the property on the tax list for the current year, except that interest or penalties must not be levied on these additional taxes if timely paid. The additional tax under this subdivision must not be imposed on that portion of the property which has actively been mined and has been removed from the program based upon the supplemental affidavits filed under subdivision 8.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1115.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Supplemental affidavits; mining activity on land.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When any portion of the property begins to be actively mined, the owner must file a supplemental affidavit within 60 days from the day any aggregate is removed stating the number of acres of the property that is actively being mined. The acres actively being mined shall be (1) valued and classified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13#stat.273.13.24">273.13, subdivision 24</a>, in the next subsequent assessment year, and (2) removed from the aggregate resource preservation property tax program under this section. The additional taxes under subdivision 7 must not be imposed on the acres that are actively being mined and have been removed from the program under this section. Copies of the original affidavit and all supplemental affidavits must be filed with the county assessor, the local zoning administrator, and the Department of Natural Resources, Division of Land and Minerals. A supplemental affidavit must be filed each time a subsequent portion of the property is actively mined, provided that the minimum acreage change is five acres, even if the actual mining activity constitutes less than five acres. Failure to file the affidavits timely shall result in the property losing its valuation deferment under this section, and additional taxes must be imposed as calculated under subdivision 7.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1115.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Lien.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The additional tax imposed by this section is a lien upon the property assessed to the same extent and for the same duration as other taxes imposed upon property within this state and, when collected, must be distributed in the manner provided by law for the collection and distribution of other property taxes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1115.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Continuation of tax treatment upon sale.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When real property qualifying under subdivision 2 is sold, additional taxes must not be extended against the property if the property continues to qualify under subdivision 2, and the new owner files an application with the assessor for continued deferment within 30 days after the sale.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.1115.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.6.21.0">2008 c 366 art 6 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.10.1.0">2009 c 88 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.57.0">2010 c 382 s 57</a>,58</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.112">
        <h1 class="shn">273.112 PRIVATE OUTDOOR RECREATIONAL, OPEN SPACE AND PARK LAND TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>This section may be cited as the "Minnesota Open Space Property Tax Law."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The present general system of ad valorem property taxation in the state of Minnesota does not provide an equitable basis for the taxation of certain private outdoor recreational, open space and park land property and has resulted in excessive taxes on some of these lands. Therefore, it is hereby declared that the public policy of this state would be best served by equalizing tax burdens upon private outdoor, recreational, open space and park land within this state through appropriate taxing measures to encourage private development of these lands which would otherwise not occur or have to be provided by governmental authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Real estate shall be entitled to valuation and tax deferment under this section only if it is:</p>
          <p>(a) actively and exclusively devoted to golf, skiing, lawn bowling, croquet, polo, or archery or firearms range recreational use or other recreational uses carried on at the establishment;</p>
          <p>(b) five acres in size or more, except in the case of a lawn bowling or croquet green or an archery or firearms range;</p>
          <p>(c)(1) operated by private individuals or, in the case of a lawn bowling or croquet green, by private individuals or corporations, and open to the public; or</p>
          <p>(2) operated by firms or corporations for the benefit of employees or guests; or</p>
          <p>(3) operated by private clubs having a membership of 50 or more or open to the public, provided that the club does not discriminate in membership requirements or selection on the basis of sex or marital status; and</p>
          <p>(d) made available for use in the case of real estate devoted to golf without discrimination on the basis of sex during the time when the facility is open to use by the public or by members, except that use for golf may be restricted on the basis of sex no more frequently than one, or part of one, weekend each calendar month for each sex and no more than two, or part of two, weekdays each week for each sex.</p>
          <p>If a golf club membership allows use of golf course facilities by more than one adult per membership, the use must be equally available to all adults entitled to use of the golf course under the membership, except that use may be restricted on the basis of sex as permitted in this section. Memberships that permit play during restricted times may be allowed only if the restricted times apply to all adults using the membership. A golf club may not offer a membership or golfing privileges to a spouse of a member that provides greater or less access to the golf course than is provided to that person's spouse under the same or a separate membership in that club, except that the terms of a membership may provide that one spouse may have no right to use the golf course at any time while the other spouse may have either limited or unlimited access to the golf course.</p>
          <p>A golf club may have or create an individual membership category which entitles a member for a reduced rate to play during restricted hours as established by the club. The club must have on record a written request by the member for such membership.</p>
          <p>A golf club that has food or beverage facilities or services must allow equal access to those facilities and services for both men and women members in all membership categories at all times. Nothing in this paragraph shall be construed to require service or access to facilities to persons under the age of 21 years or require any act that would violate law or ordinance regarding sale, consumption, or regulation of alcoholic beverages.</p>
          <p>For purposes of this subdivision and subdivision 7a, discrimination means a pattern or course of conduct and not linked to an isolated incident.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Determination of value.</span></h2>
          <p>The value of any real estate described in subdivision 3 shall upon timely application by the owner, in the manner provided in subdivision 6, be determined solely with reference to its appropriate private outdoor, recreational, open space and park land classification and value notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.03#stat.272.03.8">272.03, subdivision 8</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.11">273.11</a>. In determining such value for ad valorem tax purposes the assessor shall not consider the value such real estate would have if it were converted to commercial, industrial, residential or seasonal residential use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Valuation if requirements not met.</span></h2>
          <p>Real estate devoted to golf and operated by a private club that does not meet the requirements of subdivision 3, and is not eligible for valuation and deferment under this section, must be valued for ad valorem tax purposes by the assessor as if it were converted to commercial, industrial, residential, or seasonal residential use and were platted and available for sale as individual parcels.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Separate determination of market value and tax.</span></h2>
          <p>The assessor shall, however, make a separate determination of the market value of such real estate. The tax based upon the appropriate local tax rate applicable to such property in the taxing district shall be recorded on the property assessment records.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>Application for deferment of taxes and assessment under this section shall be made at least 60 days prior to January 2 of each year. Such application shall be filed with the assessor of the taxing district in which the real property is located on such form as may be prescribed by the commissioner of revenue. The assessor may require proof by affidavit or other written verification that the property qualifies under subdivision 3. In the case of property operated by private clubs pursuant to subdivision 3, clause (c)(3), in order to qualify for valuation and tax deferment under this section, the taxpayer must submit to the assessor proof by affidavit or other written verification that the bylaws or rules and regulations of the club meet the eligibility requirements provided under this section. The signed affidavit or other written verification shall be sufficient demonstration of eligibility for the assessor unless the county attorney determines otherwise.</p>
          <p>The county assessor shall refer any question regarding the eligibility for valuation and deferment under this section to the county attorney for advice and opinion under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/388.051#stat.388.051.1">388.051, subdivision 1</a>. Upon request of the county attorney, the taxpayer shall furnish information that the county attorney considers necessary in order to determine eligibility under this section.</p>
          <p>Real estate is not entitled to valuation and deferment under this section unless the county assessor has filed with the assessor's tax records prior to October 16 a statement that the application has been accepted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Guidelines issued by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of revenue shall develop and issue guidelines for qualification by private golf clubs under this section covering the access to and use of the golf course by members and other adults so as to be consistent with the purposes and terms of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Additional taxes.</span></h2>
          <p>When real property which is being, or has been, valued and assessed under this section no longer qualifies under subdivision 3, the portion which no longer qualifies shall be subject to additional taxes, in the amount equal to the difference between the taxes determined in accordance with subdivision 4, and the amount determined under subdivision 5, provided, however, that the amount determined under subdivision 5 shall not be greater than it would have been had the actual bona fide sale price of the real property at an arm's-length transaction been used in lieu of the market value determined under subdivision 5. The additional taxes shall be extended against the property on the tax list for the current year, provided, however, that no interest or penalties shall be levied on the additional taxes if timely paid, and provided further, that the additional taxes shall only be levied with respect to the last seven years that the property has been valued and assessed under this section. This subdivision does not apply to real property that ceases to qualify under subdivision 3 because it is acquired by the state of Minnesota or a political subdivision, agency, or instrumentality of the state, provided that the property continues to be used for a qualifying purpose for at least five years from the date that the property was acquired.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">When additional taxes not imposed.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 7, when real property ceases to qualify under subdivision 3 because of failure to comply with prohibitions against discrimination on the basis of sex, payment of additional taxes imposed under subdivision 7 is not required.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Lien.</span></h2>
          <p>The tax imposed by this section shall be a lien upon the property assessed to the same extent and for the same duration as other taxes imposed upon property within this state. The tax shall be annually extended by the county auditor and shall be collected and distributed in the manner provided by law for the collection and distribution of other property taxes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>MS 1986 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.6.53.0">1987 c 268 art 6 s 53</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.112.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Continuation of tax treatment upon transfer.</span></h2>
          <p>When title to real property qualifying under subdivision 3 is transferred, no additional taxes shall be extended against the property if (a) the property continues to qualify pursuant to subdivision 3 and (b) the purchaser files an application for continued deferment of taxes pursuant to subdivision 6 within 30 days after the sale.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.112.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1135/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 1135 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/1/#laws.2.5.0">1Sp1981 c 1 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/222/#laws.0.9.0">1983 c 222 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/412/#laws.0.1.0">1986 c 412 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.84.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 84</a>; art 6 s 5,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/277/#laws.2.20.0">1989 c 277 art 2 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.2.11.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.3.10.0">1990 c 604 art 3 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.1.13.0">1991 c 291 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.5.14.0">1993 c 375 art 5 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.5.7.0">1994 c 587 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.1.21.0">1997 c 187 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.2.14.0">1997 c 231 art 2 s 14</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.3.3.0">1998 c 389 art 3 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.1.11.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.28.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 28</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.113">
        <h1 class="shn">273.113 TAX CREDIT FOR PROPERTY IN PROPOSED BOVINE TUBERCULOSIS MODIFIED ACCREDITED ZONE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.113.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given to them:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) "bovine tuberculosis modified accredited zone" means the modified accredited zone designated by the Board of Animal Health under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/35.244">35.244</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) "located within" means that the herd is kept in the area for at least a part of calendar year 2006, 2007, or 2008; and</p>
          <p>(3) "animal" means cattle, bison, goats, and farmed Cervidae.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.113.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility; amount of credit.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Agricultural and rural vacant land classified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13#stat.273.13.23">273.13, subdivision 23</a>, located within a bovine tuberculosis modified accredited zone is eligible for a property tax credit equal to the greater of: (1) $5 per acre on the first 160 acres of the property where the herd had been located; or (2) an amount equal to $5 per acre times five acres times the highest number of animals tested on the property for bovine tuberculosis in a whole-herd test as reported by the Board of Animal Health in 2006, 2007, or 2008. The amount of the credit cannot exceed the property tax payable on the property where the herd had been located, excluding any tax attributable to residential structures. To begin to qualify for the tax credit, the owner shall file an application with the county by December 1 of the levy year. The credit must be given for each subsequent taxes payable year until the credit terminates under subdivision 4. The assessor shall indicate the amount of the property tax reduction on the property tax statement of each taxpayer receiving a credit under this section. The credit paid pursuant to this section shall be deducted from the tax due on the property as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1393">273.1393</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.113.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement for lost revenue.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The county auditor shall certify to the commissioner of revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.85#stat.270C.85.2">270C.85, subdivision 2</a>, clause (4), the amount of tax lost to the county from the property tax credit under subdivision 2. Any prior year adjustments must also be certified. The commissioner of revenue shall review the certifications to determine their accuracy. The commissioner may make the changes in the certification that are considered necessary or return a certification to the county auditor for corrections. The commissioner shall reimburse each taxing district, other than school districts, for the taxes lost. The payments must be made at the time provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.10">473H.10</a> for payment to taxing jurisdictions in the same proportion that the ad valorem tax is distributed. Reimbursements to school districts must be made as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1392">273.1392</a>. The amount necessary to make the reimbursements under this section is annually appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner of revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.113.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Termination of credit.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The credits provided under this section cease to be available beginning with taxes payable in the year following the date when the Board of Animal Health has certified that the state is free of bovine tuberculosis.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.113.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.6.22.0">2008 c 366 art 6 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.2.16.0">2009 c 88 art 2 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/389/#laws.8.8.0">2010 c 389 art 8 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.18.7.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 18 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.114">
        <h1 class="shn">273.114 RURAL PRESERVE PROPERTY TAX PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.114.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 2010 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/13/#laws.0.8.0">2011 c 13 s 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.114.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Class 2b property that had been properly enrolled under section 273.111 for taxes payable in 2008, or that is part of an agricultural homestead under section 273.13, subdivision 23, paragraph (a), at least a portion of which is enrolled under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.111">273.111</a>, is entitled to valuation and tax deferment under this section if:</p>
          <p>(1) the property is contiguous to class 2a property enrolled under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.111">273.111</a> under the same ownership;</p>
          <p>(2) there are no delinquent property taxes on the land; and</p>
          <p>(3) the property is not also enrolled for valuation and deferment under section 273.111 or 273.112, or chapter 290C or 473H.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.114.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Determination of value.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.03#stat.272.03.8">272.03, subdivision 8</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.11">273.11</a>, the value of any real estate that qualifies under subdivision 2 must, upon timely application by the owner in the manner provided in subdivision 5, not exceed the value prescribed by the commissioner of revenue for class 2a tillable property in that county. The house and garage, if any, and the immediately surrounding one acre of land and a minor, ancillary nonresidential structure, if any, shall be valued according to their appropriate value. In determining the value for ad valorem tax purposes, the assessor shall not consider the presence of commercial, industrial, residential, or seasonal recreational land use influences that may affect the value of real estate subject to this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.114.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Separate determination of market value and tax.</span></h2>
          <p>The assessor shall make a separate determination of the market value of the real estate based on its highest and best use. The tax based upon that value and the appropriate local tax rate applicable to the property in the taxing district shall be recorded on the property assessment records.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.114.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p> Application for deferment of taxes and assessment under this section shall be filed by May 1 of the year prior to the year in which the taxes are payable, provided that in calendar year 2011 the application must be filed before August 1. Any application filed under this subdivision and granted shall continue in effect for subsequent years until the property is withdrawn or no longer qualifies. The application must be filed with the assessor of the taxing district in which the real property is located on the form prescribed by the commissioner of revenue. Each application must include the most recent available aerial photograph or satellite image of the property provided by the Farm Service Agency of the United States Department of Agriculture or by the county geospatial information systems service that clearly delineates the land that is to be enrolled. The application form must contain a statement setting forth the consequences to the property owner of termination of qualification of property under the rural preserve program. The assessor may require proof by affidavit or otherwise that the property qualifies under subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.114.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Additional taxes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) When real property which is being, or has been valued and assessed under this section is sold, transferred, or no longer qualifies under subdivision 2, the portion sold, transferred, or no longer qualifying shall be subject to additional taxes in the amount equal to the difference between the taxes determined in accordance with subdivision 3 and the amount determined under subdivision 4, provided that the amount determined under subdivision 4 shall not be greater than it would have been had the actual bona fide sale price of the real property at an arm's-length transaction been used in lieu of the market value determined under subdivision 4. The additional taxes shall be extended against the property on the tax list for taxes payable in the current year, provided that no interest or penalties shall be levied on the additional taxes if timely paid and provided that the additional taxes shall only be levied with respect to the current year plus two prior years that the property has been valued and assessed under this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) In the case of a sale or transfer, the additional taxes under paragraph (a) shall not be extended against the property if the new owner submits a successful application under this section by the later of May 1 of the current year or 30 days after the sale or transfer.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For the purposes of this section, the following events do not constitute a sale or transfer for property that qualified under subdivision 2 prior to the event:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) death of a property owner when the surviving owners retain ownership of the property;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) divorce of a married couple when one of the spouses retains ownership of the property;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) marriage of a single property owner when that owner retains ownership of the property in whole or in part;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the organization or reorganization of a farm ownership entity that is not prohibited from owning agricultural land in this state under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/500.24">500.24</a>, if all owners maintain the same beneficial interest both before and after the organization or reorganization; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) transfer of the property to a trust or trustee, provided that the individual owners of the property are the grantors of the trust and they maintain the same beneficial interest both before and after placement of the property in trust.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.114.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Lien.</span></h2>
          <p>The additional tax imposed by this section shall be a lien upon the property assessed to the same extent and for the same duration as other taxes imposed on the property in this state. The tax shall be annually extended by the county auditor and if and when payable shall be collected and distributed in the manner provided by law for the collection and distribution of other property taxes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.114.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Special local assessments.</span></h2>
          <p>The payment of special local assessments levied after June 1, 2011, for improvements made to any real property described in subdivision 1 together with the interest thereon shall, on timely application as provided in subdivision 6, be deferred as long as the property meets the conditions contained in this section. If special assessments against the property have been deferred pursuant to this subdivision, the governmental unit shall file with the county recorder in the county in which the property is located a certificate containing the legal description of the affected property and of the amount deferred. When the property no longer qualifies under subdivision 1, all deferred special assessments plus interest shall be payable in equal installments spread over the time remaining until the last maturity date of the bonds issued to finance the improvement for which the assessments were levied. If the bonds have matured, the deferred special assessments plus interest shall be payable within 90 days. The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/429.061#stat.429.061.2">429.061, subdivision 2</a>, apply to the collection of these installments. A penalty shall not be levied on these special assessments if timely paid. This subdivision does not apply to special assessments levied at any time by a county or district court under chapter 116A or by a watershed district under chapter 103D.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.114.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/12/#laws.2.5.0">2009 c 12 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.2.43.0">2009 c 88 art 2 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/389/#laws.1.7.0">2010 c 389 art 1 s 7</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/13/#laws.0.2.0">2011 c 13 s 2</a>-4,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.73.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 73</a>-75; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.17.8.0">2013 c 143 art 17 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.115"><b>273.115</b> MS 1986 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.6.53.0">1987 c 268 art 6 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.116"><b>273.116</b> MS 1986 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.6.53.0">1987 c 268 art 6 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.117">
        <h1 class="shn">273.117 CONSERVATION PROPERTY TAX VALUATION.</h1>
        <p class="in">The value of real property which is subject to a conservation restriction or easement shall not be reduced by the assessor if:</p>
        <p class="in">(a) the restriction or easement is for a conservation purpose and is recorded on the property; and</p>
        <p class="in">(b) the property is being used in accordance with the terms of the conservation restriction or easement.</p>
        <p>This section does not apply to (1) conservation restrictions or easements covering riparian buffers along lakes, rivers, and streams that are used for water quantity or quality control; (2) easements in a county that has adopted, by referendum, a program to protect farmland and natural areas since 1999; or (3) conservation restrictions or easements entered into prior to May 23, 2013.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.117.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/1/#laws.2.6.0">1Sp1981 c 1 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.13.27.0">2008 c 154 art 13 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.4.17.0">2013 c 143 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/150/#laws.4.5.0">2014 c 150 art 4 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.118">
        <h1 class="shn">273.118 TAX PAID IN RECOGNITION OF CONGRESSIONAL MEDAL OF HONOR.</h1>
        <p>An owner of homestead property who submits to the commissioner of revenue a property tax statement and reasonable proof that the owner of the property:</p>
        <p>(a) is a veteran as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/197.447">197.447</a>;</p>
        <p>(b) was a resident of this state for at least six months before entering military service, or has been a resident of this state for five consecutive years before submitting the statement and proof; and</p>
        <p>(c) has been awarded the Congressional Medal of Honor;</p>
        <p class="bl">shall be paid by the commissioner of revenue, within 30 days after the commissioner receives the statement and proof, the amount of the owner's property tax liability as shown on the statement, up to $2,000. The surviving spouse of a property owner who has received a payment under this section may receive payment of property taxes under this section as long as the spouse continues to own and occupy the property for which the taxes were paid under this section and the property continues to be a homestead. Property taxes paid under this section reduce property taxes payable for purposes of chapter 290A.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.118.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.177.0">1983 c 301 s 177</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.1.46.0">1984 c 655 art 1 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.4.40.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 4 s 40</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.119">
        <h1 class="shn">273.119 CONSERVATION TAX CREDIT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.119.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Eligibility; amount of credit.</span></h2>
          <p>Land located in an agricultural preserve created under chapter 40A is eligible for a property tax credit of $1.50 per acre. To begin to qualify for the tax credit, the owner shall file with the county by January 2 of any year an application for an agricultural preserve restrictive covenant pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/40A.10#stat.40A.10.1">40A.10, subdivision 1</a>. An owner who has given notice of termination of the agricultural preserve under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/40A.11#stat.40A.11.2">40A.11, subdivision 2</a>, is not eligible for the credit. The assessor shall indicate the amount of the property tax reduction on the property tax statement of each taxpayer receiving a credit under this section. The credit paid pursuant to this section shall be deducted from the tax due on the property as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1393">273.1393</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.119.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement for lost revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>The county may transfer money from the county conservation account created in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/40A.152">40A.152</a> to the county revenue fund to reimburse the fund for the cost of the property tax credit. The county auditor shall certify to the commissioner of revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.85#stat.270C.85.2">270C.85, subdivision 2</a>, clause (4), the amount of tax lost to the county from the property tax credit under subdivision 1 and the extent that the tax lost exceeds funds available in the county conservation account. Any prior year adjustments must also be certified. The commissioner of revenue shall review the certifications to determine their accuracy. The commissioner may make the changes in the certification that are considered necessary or return a certification to the county auditor for corrections. The commissioner shall reimburse each taxing district, other than school districts, from the Minnesota conservation fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/40A.151">40A.151</a> for the taxes lost in excess of the county account. The payments must be made at the time provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.10#stat.473H.10.3">473H.10, subdivision 3</a>, for payment to taxing jurisdictions in the same proportion that the ad valorem tax is distributed.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.119.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/398/#laws.28.3.0">1986 c 398 art 28 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/313/#laws.0.8.0">1989 c 313 s 8</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.9.22.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 9 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/426/#laws.2.8.0">1990 c 426 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.3.11.0">1990 c 604 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.18.8.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 18 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.1195"><b>273.1195</b> MS 1987 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.81.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 81</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.12">
        <h1 class="shn">273.12 ASSESSMENT OF REAL PROPERTY.</h1>
        <p>It shall be the duty of every assessor and board, in estimating and determining the value of lands for the purpose of taxation, to consider and give due weight to every element and factor affecting the market value thereof, including its location with reference to roads and streets and the location of roads and streets thereon or over the same, and to take into consideration a reduction in the acreage of each tract or lot sufficient to cover the amount of land actually used for any improved public highway and the reduction in area of land caused thereby. It shall be the duty of every assessor and board, in estimating and determining the value of lands for the purpose of taxation, to consider and give due weight to lands which are comparable in character, quality, and location, to the end that all lands similarly located and improved will be assessed upon a uniform basis and without discrimination and, for agricultural lands, to consider and give recognition to its earning potential as measured by its free market rental rate.</p>
        <p>When mineral, clay, or gravel deposits exist on a property, and their extent, quality, and costs of extraction are sufficiently well known so as to influence market value, such deposits shall be recognized in valuing the property; except for mineral and energy-resource deposits which are subject to taxation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.015">298.015</a>, and except for taconite and iron-sulphide deposits which are exempt from the general property tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.25">298.25</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/11">1992-1</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1927/0/123/#laws.0.0.0">1927 c 123</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1931/0/224/#laws.0.1.0">1931 c 224 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1935/0/237/#laws.0.1.0">1935 c 237 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/574/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 574 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/427/#laws.0.2.0">1971 c 427 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/489/#laws.0.2.0">1971 c 489 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/31/#laws.23.1.0">Ex1971 c 31 art 23 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.9.7.0">1987 c 268 art 9 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.84.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.13.20.0">1989 c 329 art 13 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.1.14.0">1991 c 291 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/510/#laws.1.6.0">1994 c 510 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.8.3.0">1997 c 231 art 8 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.121">
        <h1 class="shn">273.121 VALUATION OF REAL PROPERTY, NOTICE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.121.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notice.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Any county assessor or city assessor having the powers of a county assessor, valuing or classifying taxable real property shall in each year notify those persons whose property is to be included on the assessment roll that year if the person's address is known to the assessor, otherwise the occupant of the property. The notice shall be in writing and shall be sent by ordinary mail at least ten days before the meeting of the local board of appeal and equalization under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/274.01">274.01</a> or the review process established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/274.13#stat.274.13.1c">274.13, subdivision 1c</a>. Upon written request by the owner of the property, the assessor may send the notice in electronic form or by electronic mail instead of on paper or by ordinary mail. It shall contain: (1) the market value for the current and prior assessment; (2) the market value subject to taxation after subtracting the amount of any qualifying improvements for the current assessment; (3) the classification of the property for the current and prior assessment; (4) the assessor's office address; and (5) the dates, places, and times set for the meetings of the local board of appeal and equalization, the review process established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/274.13#stat.274.13.1c">274.13, subdivision 1c</a>, and the county board of appeal and equalization. If the classification of the property has changed between the current and prior assessments, a specific note to that effect shall be prominently listed on the statement. The commissioner of revenue shall specify the form of the notice. The assessor shall attach to the assessment roll a statement that the notices required by this section have been mailed. Any assessor who is not provided sufficient funds from the assessor's governing body to provide such notices, may make application to the commissioner of revenue to finance such notices. The commissioner of revenue shall conduct an investigation and, if satisfied that the assessor does not have the necessary funds, issue a certification to the commissioner of management and budget of the amount necessary to provide such notices. The commissioner of management and budget shall issue a payment for such amount and shall deduct such amount from any state payment to such county or municipality. The necessary funds to make such payments are hereby appropriated. Failure to receive the notice shall in no way affect the validity of the assessment, the resulting tax, the procedures of any board of review or equalization, or the enforcement of delinquent taxes by statutory means.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.121.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Availability of data.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The notice must state where the information on the property is available, the times when the information may be viewed by the public, and the county's website address.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.121.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Compliance.</span></h2>
          <p>A county assessor, or a city assessor having the powers of a county assessor, who does not comply with the timely notice requirement under subdivision 1 must:</p>
          <p>(1) mail an additional valuation notice to each person who was not provided timely notice; and</p>
          <p>(2) convene a supplemental local board of appeal and equalization or local review session no sooner than ten days after sending the additional notices required by clause (1).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.121.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/31/#laws.23.2.0">Ex1971 c 31 art 23 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/363/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 363 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/437/#laws.8.7.0">1975 c 437 art 8 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/437/#laws.0.3.0">1980 c 437 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/523/#laws.23.1.0">1982 c 523 art 23 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.4.41.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 4 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.6.8.0">1988 c 719 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.5.16.0">1993 c 375 art 5 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/1/#laws.0.3.0">1995 c 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.2.17.0">1997 c 231 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.7.20.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 7 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/377/#laws.10.5.0">2002 c 377 art 10 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.13.28.0">2008 c 154 art 13 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.6.23.0">2008 c 366 art 6 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/7/#laws.5.5.0">1Sp2011 c 7 art 5 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.15.16.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 15 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.40.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.86.0">2024 c 85 s 86</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.122"><b>273.122</b> MS 1979 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/607/#laws.2.24.0">1980 c 607 art 2 s 24</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.273.123"><b>273.123</b> MS 2006 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/1/2/#laws.3.12.0">1Sp2007 c 2 art 3 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.1231">
        <h1 class="shn">273.1231 TAX RELIEF FOR DESTROYED PROPERTY; DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1231.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1231">273.1231</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1235">273.1235</a>, the following words, terms, and phrases have the meanings given them in this section unless the language or context clearly indicates that a different meaning is intended.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1231.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disaster or emergency. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Disaster or emergency" means:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a major disaster as determined by the president of the United States;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a natural disaster as determined by the secretary of agriculture;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a disaster as determined by the administrator of the Small Business Administration; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a tornado, storm, flood, earthquake, landslide, explosion, fire, or similar catastrophe, as a result of which a local emergency is declared pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/12.29">12.29</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1231.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Disaster or emergency area. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) "Disaster or emergency area" means a geographic area for which:</p>
          <p class="in">(1)(i) the president of the United States, the secretary of agriculture, or the administrator of the Small Business Administration has determined that a disaster exists pursuant to federal law, or</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) a local emergency has been declared pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/12.29">12.29</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) an application by the local unit of government requesting property tax relief under this section has been received by the governor and approved by the executive council.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The executive council must not approve an application unless:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a completed disaster survey is included; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) within the boundaries of the applicant, (i) the average damage for the buildings that are damaged is at least $5,000, and (ii) either at least 25 taxable buildings were damaged, or the total dollar amount of damage to all taxable buildings equals or exceeds one percent of the total taxable market value of buildings for the applicant as reported to the commissioner of revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.85#stat.270C.85.2">270C.85, subdivision 2</a>, clause (4), for the assessment in the year prior to the year of the damage.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1231.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Homestead property. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Homestead property" means a homestead dwelling that is classified as class 1a, 1b, or 2a property or a manufactured home or sectional home used as a homestead and taxed pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.125#stat.273.125.8">273.125, subdivision 8</a>, paragraph (b), (c), or (d).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1231.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Nonhomestead property. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Nonhomestead property" means any class of taxable real or personal property except homestead property and property that is required by law to be appraised for property tax purposes by the commissioner of revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1231.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Net tax. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Net tax" means the market value and net tax capacity taxes imposed on real and personal property under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.01">272.01</a>, including the levy under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/275.025">275.025</a>, after the subtractions listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1393">273.1393</a>, clauses (2) to (9). Net tax excludes special assessments regardless of how computed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1231.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reassessed market value. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Reassessed market value" means the taxable market value of the property established for the January 2 assessment in the year that the disaster or destruction occurs, as adjusted by the county assessor or the commissioner of revenue to reflect the loss in market value caused by the damage.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1231.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Utility property.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Utility property" means property appraised and classified for tax purposes by order of the commissioner of revenue under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.33">273.33 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.3711">273.3711</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.1231.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/1/2/#laws.3.6.0">1Sp2007 c 2 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.15.4.0">2008 c 366 art 15 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.10.2.0">2009 c 88 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/112/#laws.7.1.0">2011 c 112 art 7 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.18.9.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 18 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.1232">
        <h1 class="shn">273.1232 TAX RELIEF FOR DESTROYED PROPERTY; GENERAL PROVISIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1232.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reassessments required. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1231">273.1231 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1235">273.1235</a>, the county assessor must reassess all damaged property in a disaster or emergency area, except that the commissioner of revenue shall reassess all property for which an application is submitted to the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1233">273.1233</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1235">273.1235</a>. As soon as practical, the assessor or commissioner of revenue must report the reassessed value to the county auditor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1232.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Local tax rates. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">Except as otherwise required by law, the county auditor must compute local tax rates for taxes payable in the year following the year in which the damage occurred using the values established for the January 2 assessment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.1232.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/1/2/#laws.3.7.0">1Sp2007 c 2 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.15.6.0">2008 c 366 art 15 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.1233">
        <h1 class="shn">273.1233 TAX RELIEF FOR DESTROYED PROPERTY; LOCAL OPTION DISASTER ABATEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1233.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Abatement authorization. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/375.192">375.192</a>, a county board may grant an abatement of net tax for homestead and nonhomestead property under the provisions of this paragraph for taxes payable in the year in which the destruction occurs if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the owner submits a written application to the county assessor as soon as practical after the damage has occurred;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the owner submits a written application to the county board as soon as practical after the damage has occurred; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the county assessor determines that 50 percent or more of a homestead dwelling or other building has been (i) unintentionally or accidentally destroyed, or (ii) destroyed by arson or vandalism by someone other than the owner.</p>
          <p class="in">Abatements granted under this paragraph are not subject to approval by the commissioner of revenue.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.86">270C.86</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/375.192">375.192</a>, the commissioner of revenue may grant an abatement of net tax for utility property for taxes payable in the year in which the destruction occurs if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the owner submits a written application to the commissioner as soon as practical after the damage has occurred;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the owner forwards a copy of the written application to the county board as soon as practical after the damage has occurred; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the commissioner determines that 50 percent or more of the property has been (i) unintentionally or accidentally destroyed, or (ii) destroyed by arson or vandalism by someone other than the owner.</p>
          <p class="in">Abatements granted under this paragraph are not subject to approval by the county board of the county where the property is located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1233.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Abatement limits and allowances. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In the case of a property located within a disaster or emergency area, the abatement under this section is limited to the difference between (i) the net tax on the property computed using the market value of the property established for the January 2 assessment in the year in which the damage occurred, and (ii) the net tax computed using the reassessed value.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) In the case of property not located in a disaster or emergency area, the abatement under this section is limited to the result obtained by multiplying the difference in the net tax on the property computed using the market value of the property established for the January 2 assessment in the year in which the damage occurred, and the net tax computed using the reassessed value, times a fraction, the numerator of which is the number of months in the assessment year that the structure was not usable and the denominator of which is 12. If a structure was usable for a fraction of a month, that month is not included in the numerator.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If application is made after payment of all or a portion of the taxes being abated, the portion already paid shall be refunded to the taxpayer by the county treasurer as soon as practical.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1233.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement, levy, and appropriation. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If the destruction occurs as a result of a disaster or emergency and the property is located in a disaster or emergency area, the county auditor shall certify the abatements granted under this section to the commissioner of revenue for reimbursement to each taxing jurisdiction in which the damaged property is located. The commissioner shall make the payments to the taxing jurisdictions containing the property, other than school districts and the state, at the time distributions are made under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.10#stat.473H.10.3">473H.10, subdivision 3</a>. Reimbursements to school districts shall be made as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1392">273.1392</a>. No reimbursement is to be paid to the state treasury.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Local taxing authorities may levy in the following year the amount of unreimbursed tax dollars lost as a result of the reductions granted pursuant to this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1234">273.1234</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1235">273.1235 </a>outside of any statutory restriction as to levy amount or tax rate.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) There is annually appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner of revenue an amount necessary to make the payments required by this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.1233.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/1/2/#laws.3.8.0">1Sp2007 c 2 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.15.7.0">2008 c 366 art 15 s 7</a>,8</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.1234">
        <h1 class="shn">273.1234 TAX RELIEF FOR DESTROYED PROPERTY; HOMESTEAD AND DISASTER CREDITS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1234.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Credit provided. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">The county auditor shall compute a credit for taxes payable in the year following the year in which the damage or destruction occurred for each reassessed homestead property within the county that is located within a disaster or emergency area. The credit is equal to the difference in the net tax on the property computed using the market value of the property established for the January 2 assessment in the year in which the damage occurred and as computed using the reassessed value.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1234.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Credit reimbursements. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">The county auditor shall certify the credits granted under this section to the commissioner of revenue for reimbursement to each taxing jurisdiction in which the damaged property is located. The commissioner shall make the payments to the taxing jurisdictions containing the property, other than school districts and the state, at the time distributions are made under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.10">473H.10</a>, subdivision 3. Reimbursements to school districts shall be made as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1392">273.1392</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1234.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriation. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">There is annually appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner of revenue an amount necessary to make the payments required by this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.1234.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/1/2/#laws.3.9.0">1Sp2007 c 2 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.15.9.0">2008 c 366 art 15 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.1235">
        <h1 class="shn">273.1235 TAX RELIEF FOR DESTROYED PROPERTY; LOCAL OPTION DISASTER CREDITS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1235.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Credit provided. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">The county board may grant a credit for taxes payable in the year following the year in which the damage or destruction occurred for: (1) homestead property that meets all the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1233#stat.273.1233.1">273.1233, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), but that does not qualify for a credit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1234">273.1234</a>, except that an application need only be submitted by the end of the year in which the damage occurred; and (2) nonhomestead and utility property that meets all the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1233">273.1233</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (b), except that an application need only be submitted by the end of the year in which the damage occurred.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1235.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Credit calculation. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">In the case of a property located within a disaster or emergency area, the credit is equal to the difference between (i) the net tax on the property computed using the market value of the property established for the January 2 assessment in the year in which the damage occurred, and (ii) the net tax computed using the reassessed value. In the case of property not located in a disaster or emergency area, the credit under this section is equal to the result obtained by multiplying the difference in the net tax on the property computed using the market value of the property established for the January 2 assessment in the year in which the damage occurred, and the net tax computed using the reassessed value, times a fraction, the numerator of which is the number of months in the assessment year that the structure was not usable and the denominator of which is 12. If a structure was usable for a fraction of a month, that month is not included in the numerator.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1235.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Credit reimbursements. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">The county auditor shall certify the credits granted under this section for property within a disaster or emergency area to the commissioner of revenue for reimbursement to each taxing jurisdiction in which the damaged property is located. The commissioner shall make the payments to the taxing jurisdictions containing the property, other than school districts and the state, at the time distributions are made under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.10#stat.473H.10.3">473H.10, subdivision 3</a>. Reimbursements to school districts shall be made as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1392">273.1392</a>. No reimbursement is to be made for credits to property not located in a disaster or emergency area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.1235.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Appropriation. </span></h2>
          <p class="in">There is annually appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner of revenue an amount necessary to make the payments required by this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.273.1235.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/1/2/#laws.3.10.0">1Sp2007 c 2 art 3 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.15.10.0">2008 c 366 art 15 s 10</a>,11</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.273.124">
        <h1 class="shn">273.124 HOMESTEAD DETERMINATION; SPECIAL RULES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.273.124.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General rule.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Residential real estate that is occupied and used for the purposes of a homestead by its owner, who must be a Minnesota resident, is a residential homestead.</p>
          <p class="in">Agricultural land, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13#stat.273.13.23">273.13, subdivision 23</a>, that is occupied and used as a homestead by its owner, who must be a Minnesota resident, is an agricultural homestead.</p>
          <p class="in">Dates for establishment of a homestead and homestead treatment provided to particular types of property are as provided in this section.</p>
          <p class="in">Property held by a trustee under a trust is eligible for homestead classification if the requirements under this chapter are satisfied.</p>
          <p class="in">The assessor shall require proof, as provided in subdivision 13, of the facts upon which classification as a homestead may be determined. Notwithstanding any other law, the assessor may at any time require a homestead application to be filed in order to verify that any property classified as a homestead continues to be eligible for homestead status. Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, the Department of Revenue may, upon request from an assessor, verify whether an individual who is requesting or receiving homestead classification has filed a Minnesota income tax return as a resident for the most recent taxable year for which the information is available.</p>
          <p class="in">When there is a name change or a transfer of homestead property, the assessor may reclassify the property in the next assessment unless a homestead application is filed to verify that the property continues to qualify for homestead classification.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For purposes of this section, homestead property shall include property which is used for purposes of the homestead but is separated from the homestead by a road, street, lot, waterway, or other similar intervening property. The term "used for purposes of the homestead" shall include but not be limited to uses for gardens, garages, or other outbuildings commonly associated with a homestead, but shall not include vacant land held primarily for future development. In order to receive homestead treatment for the noncontiguous property, the owner must use the property for the purposes of the homestead, and must apply to the assessor, both by the deadlines given in subdivision 9. After initial qualification for the homestead treatment, additional applications for subsequent years are not required.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Residential real estate that is occupied and used for purposes of a homestead by a relative of the owner is a homestead but only to the extent of the homestead treatment that would be provided if the related owner occupied the property. For purposes of this paragraph and paragraph (g), "relative" means a parent, stepparent, child, stepchild, grandparent, grandchild, brother, sister, uncle, aunt, nephew, or niece. This relationship may be by blood or marriage. Property that has been classified as seasonal residential recreational property at any time during which it has been owned by the current owner or spouse of the current owner will not be reclassified as a homestead unless it is occupied as a homestead by the owner; this prohibition also applies to property that, in the absence of this paragraph, would have been classified as seasonal residential recreational property at the time when the residence was constructed. Neither the related occupant nor the owner of the property may claim a property tax refund under chapter 290A for a homestead occupied by a relative. In the case of a residence located on agricultural land, only the house, garage, and immediately surrounding one acre of land shall be classified as a homestead under this paragraph, except as provided in paragraph (d).</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Agricultural property that is occupied and used for purposes of a homestead by a relative of the owner, is a homestead, only to the extent of the homestead treatment that would be provided if the related owner occupied the property, and only if all of the following criteria are met:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the relative who is occupying the agricultural property is a grandchild, child, sibling, parent, grandparent, stepparent, stepchild, uncle, aunt, nephew, or niece of the owner of the agricultural property or of the spouse of the owner;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the owner of the agricultural property must be a Minnesota resident;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the owner of the agricultural property must not receive homestead treatment on any other agricultural property in Minnesota; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the owner of the agricultural property is limited to only one agricultural homestead per family under this paragraph.</p>
          <p class="in">Neither the related occupant nor the owner of the property may claim a property tax refund under chapter 290A for a homestead occupied by a relative qualifying under this paragraph. For purposes of this paragraph, "agricultural property" means the house, garage, other farm buildings and structures, and agricultural land.</p>
          <p class="in">Application must be made to the assessor by the owner of the agricultural property to receive homestead benefits under this paragraph. The assessor may require the necessary proof that the requirements under this paragraph have been met.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) In the case of property owned by a property owner who is married, the assessor must not deny homestead treatment in whole or in part if only one of the spouses occupies the property and the other spouse is absent due to: (1) marriage dissolution proceedings, (2) legal separation, (3) employment or self-employment in another location, or (4) other personal circumstances causing the spouses to live separately, not including an intent to obtain two homestead classifications for property tax purposes. To qualify under clause (3), the spouse's place of employment or self-employment must be at least 50 miles distant from the other spouse's place of employment, and the homesteads must be at least 50 miles distant from each other.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The assessor must not deny homestead treatment in whole or in part if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) in the case of a property owner who is not married, the owner is absent due to residence in a nursing home, boarding care facility, or an elderly assisted living facility property as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13#stat.273.13.25a">273.13, subdivision 25a</a>, and the property is not otherwise occupied; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) in the case of a property owner who is married, the owner or the owner's spouse or both are absent due to residence in a nursing home, boarding care facility, or an elderly assisted living facility property as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13#stat.273.13.25a">273.13, subdivision 25a</a>, and the property is not occupied or is occupied only by the owner's spouse.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) If an individual is purchasing property with the intent of claiming it as a homestead and is required by the terms of the financing agreement to have a relative shown on the deed as a co-owner, the assessor shall allow a full homestead classification. This provision only applies to first-time purchasers, whether married or single, or to a person who had previously been married and is purchasing as a single individual for the first time. The application for homestead benefits must be on a form prescribed by the commissioner and must contain the data necessary for the assessor to determine if full homestead benefits are warranted.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) If residential or agricultural real estate is occupied and used for purposes of a homestead by a child of a deceased owner and the property is subject to jurisdiction of probate court, the child shall receive relative homestead classification under paragraph (c) or (d) to the same extent they would be entitled to it if the owner was still living, until the probate is completed. For purposes of this paragraph, "child" includes a relationship by blood or by marriage.</p>
